user
You are the second reviewer on a routine security code review of our own
nix-bitcoin fork: the NixOS modules and packaging we deploy on our own Bitcoin
and Lightning node. Two prior reviewers have already produced the findings
below against the exact commit checked out in the current directory. Your job
is quality control on their work, as the maintainer's independent check:

For EACH finding, open the cited file at the cited location and decide:
- verdict: "confirmed" (the defect is real as described), "false-positive"
  (the code does not do what the finding claims, or an existing control
  already prevents it — name the control), or "needs-info" (cannot be decided
  from the tree; say what is missing).
- severity: your own call (critical/high/medium/low/info), which may differ
  from the reviewer's.
- reasoning: one to three sentences grounded in the code you read (quote the
  line or construct that decides it).
- fix_ok: whether the reviewer's recommended fix is correct and complete
  (true/false) and, if false, what is wrong with it.

Then, only for the files the findings touch, note up to three defects the
reviewers missed or misread that a maintainer should know about ("missed").
Do not pad: if there is nothing, say so.

Read-only tools only; do not modify the tree. Be terse and concrete.

End your answer with exactly one fenced ```json block of this shape:
{"reviews":[{"id":"<finding id or title>","verdict":"confirmed|false-positive|needs-info","severity":"...","reasoning":"...","fix_ok":true,"fix_note":""}],
 "missed":[{"file":"...","line":0,"severity":"...","title":"...","description":"..."}],
 "summary":"two or three sentences"}
followed by nothing else.

  {
    "id": "F1",
    "title": "lnd custom macaroons inherit the target user's login group, leaking to shared 'users' group",
    "severity": "low",
    "confidence": null,
    "model": "kimi-k3",
    "file": "modules/lnd.nix",
    "line": 300,
    "description": "The root ExecStartPost creates /run/lnd/<name>.macaroon as root:root 0440, then runs `chown <user>:` (empty group). Per chown(1), a trailing colon with no group sets the group to the user's LOGIN group. For a macaroon granted to a normal login user (NixOS default primary group 'users', gid 100), the macaroon becomes readable by every normal user on the system, widening the grant beyond the named user. Shipped config is unaffected (the only defined macaroon targets system user 'btcpayserver', whose login group is its own), so this is config-dependent: it bites only when an operator defines services.lnd.macaroons.<n>.user as a regular login account. /run/lnd is mode 711, so group members can traverse to the file.",
    "impact": "A macaroon intended for one login user is readable by all users in group 'users'; depending on the macaroon's permissions (e.g. invoices:write, onchain:read) this is an unintended privilege/隐私 grant.",
    "recommendation": "Resolve the group explicitly instead of relying on login-group semantics: e.g. a dedicated per-macaroon group, or `chown <user>:nogroup` plus an ACL, or document that macaroon `user` must be a system user with a same-named group."
  },
  {
    "id": "F2",
    "title": "update-nix-bitcoin auto-selects the newest unsigned release tag; integrity rests on advisory operator review",
    "severity": "low",
    "confidence": null,
    "model": "kimi-k3",
    "file": "helper/makeShell.nix",
    "line": 74,
    "description": "update-nix-bitcoin picks the highest CalVer tag via `git ls-remote --tags ... | sort -V | tail -n1`, resolves it to a commit with two further ls-remote calls (re-targetable between calls), prefetches that commit's tarball, and writes the pin. There is no signature check on the tag or commit, although commits are SSH-signed (SECURITY.md documents tags as unsigned CI pointers). The written pin is self-consistent (commit + prefetched narHash) and the helper correctly refuses to auto-exec the new shell, printing a compare URL. But nothing forces the review: an operator who skips it and re-enters nix-shell evaluates the newly pinned tree's shellHook. This is weaker than the refused upstream helper (helper/fetch-release), which GPG-verifies the nar-hash against a pinned key. Repo/tag compromise (or a maliciously pushed '20*' tag by anyone with tag-push rights) flows into the pin with only a human gate.",
    "impact": "Supply-chain: a re-pointed or attacker-created tag yields an operator-approved-looking pin of unreviewed code. Mitigated by commit pinning, the printed compare URL, and no automatic shell re-entry.",
    "recommendation": "Verify the resolved commit's SSH signature against the maintainer's allowed-signers entry before writing the pin (the infrastructure exists: commits are signed), or at minimum fail unless the tag is an annotated tag object created by the CI workflow identity."
  },

    "id": "F3",
    "title": "netns-exec leaks the netns fd into the executed program (missing O_CLOEXEC)",
    "severity": "informational",
    "confidence": null,
    "model": "kimi-k3",
    "file": "pkgs/netns-exec/src/main.c",
    "line": 73,
    "description": "`open(netns_path, O_RDONLY)` lacks O_CLOEXEC, so the fd survives execv into the operator-chosen program running inside the namespace. The exec'd process runs as the unprivileged operator with all capabilities dropped, and setns() on the leaked fd requires CAP_SYS_ADMIN, so no privilege gain was found; if the exec target is itself setuid/file-capable, that target already has the powers the fd would grant. Pure hygiene.",
    "impact": "None demonstrated; leaked fd is unusable without CAP_SYS_ADMIN.",
    "recommendation": "open(netns_path, O_RDONLY | O_CLOEXEC)."
  },

Since the last commit, evaluation refuses a disabled firewall and the nftables backend, closing the two silent-failure paths, and the accept rule is interface- and single-peer-scoped. Residual: any future local netfilter misconfiguration (or a local process binding/forwarding) exposes the macaroon-admin API; there is no defense in depth at the bind layer. The rebind to the WG server address is deliberately deferred with a breakage checklist in docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md, and lnd's restlisten=0.0.0.0 is IPv4-only so there is no parallel IPv6 gap.",
    "impact": "Single-point-of-failure on iptables for an admin-grade API; currently guarded and CI-gated (wireguard-lndconnect scenario).",
    "recommendation": "Work through docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md and bind to the WG server address; until then, consider also asserting services.lnd.restAddress == 0.0.0.0 only when the accept rule is in place."

   266	            # so this root script must never write/chmod/chown through a path the
   267	            # lnd process could tamper with (else a compromised lnd escalates to
   268	            # arbitrary root file overwrite/chown). Staging in $RUNTIME_DIRECTORY
   269	            # is NOT enough — lnd owns that directory and can unlink our temp file
   270	            # and swap in a symlink before we write to it. Stage instead in a
   271	            # ROOT-ONLY directory (mode 0700, owned by root, that lnd cannot enter
   272	            # or replace) on the SAME tmpfs as /run/lnd, so the final move is a
   273	            # rename(2): atomic, and it replaces the destination without following
   274	            # a symlink lnd may have planted there.
   275	            staging=$(${pkgs.coreutils}/bin/mktemp -d "/run/lnd-macaroons.XXXXXX")
   276	            trap '${pkgs.coreutils}/bin/rm -rf "$staging"' EXIT
   277	            ${lib.concatMapStrings (macaroon: ''
   278	              echo "Create custom macaroon ${macaroon}"
   279	              macaroonTmp="$staging/${macaroon}.macaroon"
   280	              # Read admin.macaroon AS the lnd user, never as root: it lives in
   281	              # lnd's own 0770 datadir, so a compromised lnd could plant a
   282	              # symlink there and have this root script dereference it to
   283	              # exfiltrate an arbitrary root-only file (backup passphrase, wg
   284	              # key, host keys) via the REST call below. Reading as the lnd user
   285	              # fails closed on such a symlink — lnd cannot read what lnd cannot
   286	              # read — and grants no access lnd does not already have. (The write
   287	              # path above is already symlink-hardened; this closes the read.)
   288	              adminMacaroonHex=$(${pkgs.util-linux}/bin/runuser -u ${cfg.user} -- ${pkgs.xxd}/bin/xxd -ps -u -c 99999 '${networkDir}/admin.macaroon')
   289	              # The response body is lnd-controlled. A macaroon is a few hundred
   290	              # bytes, so cap what this root script will buffer and write to the
   291	              # /run tmpfs at 64 KiB, and fail (jq -e) rather than write garbage
   292	              # when the reply carries no `.macaroon` (audit 2026-08-30, low).
   293	              ${curl} \
   294	                -H @<(printf 'Grpc-Metadata-macaroon: %s\n' "$adminMacaroonHex") \
   295	                -X POST \
   296	                -d '{"permissions":[${cfg.macaroons.${macaroon}.permissions}]}' \
   297	                ${restUrl}/macaroon |\
   298	                ${pkgs.coreutils}/bin/head -c 65536 |\
   299	                ${pkgs.jq}/bin/jq -ce '.macaroon | strings' | ${pkgs.xxd}/bin/xxd -p -r > "$macaroonTmp"
   300	              chown ${cfg.macaroons.${macaroon}.user}: "$macaroonTmp"
   301	              # -T: the destination is a path lnd controls; without -T a
   302	              # symlink-to-directory planted there would make root move the
   303	              # file INTO that directory under the fixed basename instead of
   304	              # replacing the path (second opinion 2026-09-09).
   305	              ${pkgs.coreutils}/bin/mv -fT -- "$macaroonTmp" "$RUNTIME_DIRECTORY/${macaroon}.macaroon"
   306	            '') (attrNames cfg.macaroons)}
   307	          '';
   308	        in [
   309	          script
   310	        ];
   311	      } // nbLib.allowedIPAddresses cfg.tor.enforce;
   312	    };
   313	
   314	    users.users.${cfg.user} = {
   315	      isSystemUser = true;
   316	      group = cfg.group;
   317	      extraGroups = [ "bitcoinrpc-public" ];
   318	      home = cfg.dataDir; # lnd creates .lnd dir in HOME
   319	    };
   320	    users.groups.${cfg.group} = {};
   321	    nix-bitcoin.operator = {
   322	      groups = [ cfg.group ];
   323	      allowRunAsUsers = [ cfg.user ];
   324	    };
   325	
   326	    nix-bitcoin.secrets = {
   327	      lnd-wallet-password.user = cfg.user;
   328	      lnd-key.user = cfg.user;
   329	      lnd-cert.user = cfg.user;
   330	      lnd-cert.permissions = "444"; # world readable
   331	    };
   332	    # Advantages of manually pre-generating certs:
   333	    # - Reduces dynamic state
   334	    # - Enables deployment of a mesh of server plus client nodes with predefined certs
   335	    nix-bitcoin.generateSecretsCmds.lnd = ''
   336	      makePasswordSecret lnd-wallet-password
   337	      makeCert lnd '${nbLib.mkCertExtraAltNames cfg.certificate}'
   338	    '';
   339	  };
   340	}

   464	      group = cfg.group;
   465	    };
   466	    users.groups.${cfg.group} = {};
   467	    users.groups.bitcoinrpc-public = {};
   468	
   469	    nix-bitcoin.operator.groups = [ cfg.group ];
   470	
   471	    nix-bitcoin.secrets = {
   472	      bitcoin-rpcpassword-privileged.user = cfg.user;
   473	      bitcoin-rpcpassword-public = {
   474	        user = cfg.user;
   475	        group = "bitcoinrpc-public";
   476	      };
   477	
   478	      bitcoin-HMAC-privileged.user = cfg.user;
   479	      bitcoin-HMAC-public.user = cfg.user;
   480	    };
   481	    nix-bitcoin.generateSecretsCmds.bitcoind = ''
   482	      makeBitcoinRPCPassword privileged
   483	      makeBitcoinRPCPassword public
   484	    '';
   485	  };
   486	}
     1	# Security Policy
     2	
     3	This repository is an independently maintained fork of
     4	[fort-nix/nix-bitcoin](https://github.com/fort-nix/nix-bitcoin), which is no
     5	longer actively maintained. The contacts, GPG keys, signing key, and security
     6	fund listed in upstream's SECURITY.md belong to the original developers and do
     7	not reach or represent this fork. Do not send reports or donations to them on
     8	our behalf.
     9	
    10	## Reporting a Vulnerability
    11	
    12	Report vulnerabilities through [GitHub private vulnerability
    13	reporting](../../security/advisories/new). This opens a private security
    14	advisory that only the maintainers can see.
    15	
    16	If private reporting is unavailable, open a regular issue that says you have a
    17	security concern, without any details, and we will arrange a private channel.
    18	
    19	Please include:
    20	
    21	- The component and version or commit you tested
    22	- Steps to reproduce or a proof of concept
    23	- The impact you see (fund theft, privacy loss, denial of service, ...)
    24	
    25	We aim to acknowledge reports within 7 days. We follow coordinated disclosure:
    26	we agree on a public disclosure date with you, credit you in the fix and
    27	release notes unless you prefer anonymity.
    28	
    29	## Scope
    30	
    31	In scope:
    32	
    33	- nix-bitcoin's own code: NixOS modules, `netns-exec`, secrets handling,
    34	  helper scripts, packaging in `pkgs/`
    35	- Privilege boundaries we claim to provide: service isolation, RPC
    36	  whitelisting, `netns-isolation`, systemd sandboxing
    37	- Documentation that instructs users to do something blatantly insecure
    38	
    39	Out of scope:
    40	
    41	- Vulnerabilities in the daemons we package (bitcoind, lnd,
    42	  electrs, btcpayserver, nbxplorer, ...). Report those to the upstream
    43	  project first. If upstream has shipped a fix that we have not yet pulled in,
    44	  tell us and we will treat it as urgent.
    45	- Attacks requiring physical access or an already-compromised deployment
    46	  machine.
    47	
    48	## Supported Versions
    49	
    50	Only the latest commit on the default branch and the latest release are
    51	supported. Security fixes are delivered by updating your flake input; we do
    52	not maintain stable branches.
    53	
    54	## Security Tracker
    55	
    56	Known findings and their status are tracked in
    57	[docs/security-audit-2026-08-14.md](docs/security-audit-2026-08-14.md). The
    58	audit is re-run on every nixpkgs pin update.
    59	
    60	## Adversarial review
    61	
    62	Beyond CI's NixOS VM assertion suite, every change is reviewed by two
    63	independent open-weight models (Kimi K3 + GLM-5.2) acting as adversarial
    64	security auditors, at max reasoning. The **methodology, the audit tooling, and
    65	the full results of every run** live on the [`audits`](../../tree/audits)
    66	branch — kept off `master`/`release` so the code you pin stays clean. Browse it
    67	to follow the reviews, read the raw transcripts, or run the suite yourself;
    68	PRs to improve the audit prompt are welcome.
    69	
    70	## Release Integrity
    71	
    72	Commits on this fork are signed with the maintainer's SSH key. GitHub shows
    73	them as "Verified"; to verify locally, add the maintainer's public signing key
    74	to an SSH allowed-signers file and run `git log --show-signature` or
    75	`git verify-commit <rev>`. The CalVer release tags are created by CI
    76	(`release-tag.yml`) as annotated but **unsigned** tags — a tag name is a
    77	convenience pointer, not a signature. Verify the *commit* a tag points at, and
    78	pin by commit hash + `narHash` (below) rather than by tag name.
    79	
    80	Because this fork is consumed as a flake input, integrity ultimately comes
    81	from your `flake.lock`: it records the exact commit and its content hash
    82	(`narHash`), and Nix refuses any input whose contents do not match. Pin a
    83	specific revision and review changes before advancing it.
    84	
    85	`bitcoind` source tarballs are additionally verified against the Bitcoin
    86	Core `guix.sigs` keyring at build time.
    87	
    88	Note: the upstream tarball-release helper `helper/fetch-release` is not used by
    89	this fork. It fetches upstream fort-nix releases and references the original
    90	maintainer's GPG key. It refuses to run unless `NIX_BITCOIN_FETCH_UPSTREAM=1`
    91	is set, and it points at the flake pin instead. Do not rely on it. This fork
    92	releases through CalVer tags on the `release` branch (`release-tag.yml`).

    52	    text=
    53	    host=
    54	    for arg in "$@"; do
    55	      case $arg in
    56	        --text)

    58	          ;;
    59	        *)
    60	          host=$arg
    61	          ;;
    62	      esac
    63	    done
    64

    68	      # queries a set of external ip providers.

    74	    fi

    77	    PrivateKey = $(cat ${secretsDir}/wg-peer-private-key)
    78	    Address = ${peerAddress}/24

    80	    [Peer]

    83	    Endpoint = $host:${toString wg-nb.listenPort}
    84	    PersistentKeepalive = 25
    85	    "
    86	
    87	    if [[ $text ]]; then

    89	    else
    90	      echo "$config" | ${getExe pkgs.qrencode} -t UTF8 -o -
    91	    fi
    92	  '';
    93	in {
    94	  inherit options;
    95	
    96	  config = {
    97	    assertions = [
    98	      {
    99	        # Don't support `netns-isolation` for now to keep things simple
   100	        assertion = !(config.nix-bitcoin.netns-isolation.enable or false);
   101	        message = "`nix-bitcoin.wireguard` is not compatible with `netns-isolation`.";
   102	      }
   103	      # The whole security model of this preset is the iptables rule set
   104	      # below: the lnd admin REST listener is bound to 0.0.0.0 (see the comment
   105	      # at `restAddress`) and the peer restriction is an iptables REJECT. Two
   106	      # silent ways to lose that (audit 2026-09-09): the firewall disabled, or
   107	      # the nftables backend enabled — NixOS then ignores `extraCommands`
   108	      # without an error, so the REST accept rule and the restrictPeer REJECT
   109	      # simply vanish. Refuse to evaluate rather than ship an unguarded admin API.
   110	      {
   111	        assertion = !lndconnect || config.networking.firewall.enable;
   112	        message = "nix-bitcoin wireguard preset: services.lnd.lndconnect binds the admin REST API to 0.0.0.0 and relies on networking.firewall — enable the firewall.";
   113	      }
   114	      {
   115	        assertion = !config.networking.nftables.enable;
   116	        message = "nix-bitcoin wireguard preset: its rules are iptables `extraCommands`, which the nftables backend silently drops (networking.nftables.enable = true). Keep the iptables backend or port the rules.";
   117	      }
   118	    ];
   119	
   120	    networking.wireguard.interfaces.wg-nb = {
   121	      ips = [ "${serverAddress}/24" ];
   122	      listenPort = mkDefault 51820;
   123	      privateKeyFile = "${secretsDir}/wg-server-private-key";
   124	      allowedIPsAsRoutes = false;
   125	      peers = [
   126	        {
   127	          # To use the actual public key from the secrets file, use dummy pubkey
   128	          # `peer0` and replace it via `getPubkeyFromFile` (see further below)
   129	          # at peer service runtime.
   130	          publicKey = "peer0";
   131	          allowedIPs = [ "${peerAddress}/32" ];
   132	        }
   133	      ];
   134	    };
   135	
   136	    systemd.services = {
   137	      wireguard-wg-nb = rec {
   138	        wants = [ "nix-bitcoin-secrets.target" ];
   139	        after = wants;
   140	      };
   141	
   142	      # Read the peer pubkey from the secrets file at service runtime.
   143	      # (Upstream patched its own source with sed and re-executed it as root,
   144	      # which turned a secrets-store write into root RCE; audit L-8.)
   145	      wireguard-wg-nb-peer-peer0 = {
   146	        script = mkForce ''
   147	          ${pkgs.wireguard-tools}/bin/wg set wg-nb peer "$(cat "${secretsDir}/wg-peer-public-key")" allowed-ips ${peerAddress}/32
   148	        '';
   149	        postStop = mkForce ''
   150	          ${pkgs.wireguard-tools}/bin/wg set wg-nb peer "$(cat "${secretsDir}/wg-peer-public-key")" remove || true
   151	        '';

   155	    environment.systemPackages = [
   156	      wgConnect
   157	    ] ++ (optional lndconnect
   158	      (pkgs.writers.writeBashBin "lndconnect-wg" ''
   159	        exec lndconnect --host "${serverAddress}" --nocert "$@"
   160	      '')
   161	    );
   162	
   163	    networking.firewall = let
   164	      restrictPeerRule = "-s ${peerAddress} ! -d ${serverAddress} -j REJECT";
   165	    in {
   166	      allowedUDPPorts = [ wg-nb.listenPort ];
   167	
   168	      extraCommands =
   169	        optionalString lndconnect ''
   170	          # Admit lnd REST only from the single WireGuard peer, and only when the
   171	          # packet actually arrived over the wg-nb interface. `-s` alone matches
   172	          # a spoofed source on any interface; `-i` pins it to the tunnel (audit
   173	          # 2026-08-29, low). This is the interim hardening while the
   174	          # 0.0.0.0 bind itself stays deferred (see docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md).
   175	          iptables -w -A nixos-fw -i wg-nb -p tcp -s ${peerAddress}/32 --dport ${toString lnd.restPort} -j nixos-fw-accept
   176	        ''
   177	        + optionalString cfg.restrictPeer ''
   178	          # Insert at the top: appended (-A) the REJECT would sit behind the
   179	          # standard accept rules (allowed ports, the REST accept above), so a
   180	          # peer could still reach any firewall-allowed port on the node's
   181	          # other addresses through the tunnel (second opinion 2026-09-09).
   182	          # Replies to server-initiated traffic are unaffected: their
   183	          # destination is serverAddress.
   184	          iptables -w -I nixos-fw 1 ${restrictPeerRule}
   185	          iptables -w -I FORWARD 1 ${restrictPeerRule}
   186	        '';
   187	
   188	      extraStopCommands =
   189	        # Rules added to chain `nixos-fw` are automatically removed when restarting
   190	        # the NixOS firewall service.
   191	        mkIf cfg.restrictPeer ''
   192	          iptables -w -D FORWARD ${restrictPeerRule} || :
   193	        '';
   194	    };
   195	
   196	    # Listen on all addresses, including `serverAddress`.
   197	    # The listen port is guarded by the firewall (nixos-fw default-drops; only
   198	    # the wg subnet accept rule above admits REST traffic), but this is still
   199	    # a known audit finding (2026-08-24, advisory): the admin REST API relies
   200	    # on the firewall alone. Binding to `serverAddress` instead is DEFERRED —
   201	    # `nbLib.address` maps 0.0.0.0 to 127.0.0.1, so every internal consumer
   202	    # of `restAddress` (macaroon ExecStartPost, btcpayserver, lndconnect
   203	    # onion target) silently depends on this value, and a naive rebind breaks
   204	    # them plus lnd startup ordering. Read docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md and
   205	    # work through its checklist before changing this line.
   206	    services.lnd = mkIf lndconnect {
   207	      restAddress = "0.0.0.0";
   208	      tor.enforce = false;
   209	    };

   211	    nix-bitcoin.secrets = {
   212	      wg-server-private-key = {};
   213	      wg-server-public-key = { user = wgConnectUser; group = "root"; };
   214	      wg-peer-private-key = { user = wgConnectUser; group = "root"; };
   215	      wg-peer-public-key = {};
   216	    };
   217	
   218	    nix-bitcoin.generateSecretsCmds.wireguard = let
   219	      wg = "${pkgs.wireguard-tools}/bin/wg";
   220	    in ''
   221	      makeWireguardKey() {
   222	        local name=$1
   223	        local priv=wg-$name-private-key
   224	        local pub=wg-$name-public-key
   225	        if [[ ! -e $priv ]]; then
   226	          ${wg} genkey > $priv
   227	        fi
   228	        if [[ $priv -nt $pub ]]; then
   229	          ${wg} pubkey < $priv > $pub
   230	        fi
   231	      }
   232	      makeWireguardKey server
   233	      makeWireguardKey peer
   234	    '';
   235	  };
   236	}

     4	# transaction relay, no leaks of peer topology or wallet contents.

     8	#   trivially abused for DoS by any holder of the public credentials.
     9	# - getblockfrompeer: lets the caller drive the node's P2P block fetching.
    10	# - fundrawtransaction: operates on a loaded bitcoind wallet — it selects and
    11	#   locks UTXOs and reveals change/wallet contents, violating the wallet-safety
    12	#   bar above. No local consumer (lnd, electrs, nbxplorer) uses it; lnd/nbxplorer
    13	#   manage their own wallets and electrs only reads blocks. Removed in the audit
    14	#   follow-up 2026-08-20.

    21	# them makes lnd fail its health check and shut itself down. They only leak

     1	# Deferred: bind lnd's REST API to the WireGuard address
     2	
     3	Status: **DEFERRED — do not implement without working through every item below.**
     4	
     5	The 2026-08-24 adversarial audit (run `2026-08-24-111031`, both models) flagged
     6	that the wireguard preset sets `services.lnd.restAddress = "0.0.0.0"`
     7	(modules/presets/wireguard.nix), exposing the macaroon-admin REST API on all
     8	interfaces and relying solely on the nixos-fw firewall (default-drop plus an
     9	accept rule for the wg subnet) for protection. Severity medium/low, advisory.
    10	The suggested fix — bind to the wg server address (`${subnet}.1`, default
    11	10.10.0.1) instead — is correct as defense-in-depth but **breaks several
    12	things that are non-obvious**, because of one load-bearing detail:
    13	
    14	> `nbLib.address` (pkgs/lib.nix) maps the bind address `0.0.0.0` to the
    15	> *connect* address `127.0.0.1`. Every internal consumer of `restAddress`
    16	> goes through it. Today they all silently talk to localhost, which the
    17	> default TLS cert covers. Bind to `10.10.0.1` and they **all** follow.
    18	
    19	## What a naive `restAddress = serverAddress` breaks
    20	
    21	1. **lnd startup becomes hard-coupled to WireGuard.** lnd fails fatally if it
    22	   cannot bind `restlisten`; the `10.10.0.1` address only exists once
    23	   `wireguard-wg-nb.service` has configured the interface. Without systemd
    24	   ordering, boot is a crash-loop race (`Restart=on-failure`/10s masks it
    25	   until it doesn't). *With* ordering, a broken wg setup (e.g. missing
    26	   `wg-server-private-key` secret) takes the whole lightning node down —
    27	   today lnd runs fine with wg broken. This coupling is inherent to the fix;
    28	   it must be a conscious acceptance, or defused with
    29	   `net.ipv4.ip_nonlocal_bind = 1` (system-wide sysctl loosening — an
    30	   auditor trade-off of its own).
    31	
    32	2. **TLS cert SANs.** The generated lnd cert only has
    33	   `DNS:localhost,IP:127.0.0.1` plus `rpcAddress` when non-local
    34	   (modules/secrets/secrets.nix `doMakeCert`; modules/lnd.nix
    35	   `certificate.extraIPs`). Nothing adds the REST address. Any validating
    36	   client connecting to `https://10.10.0.1:8080` fails TLS. The preset must
    37	   set `services.lnd.certificate.extraIPs = [ serverAddress ]`. Note
    38	   `makeCert` *does* regenerate the cert when alt-names change
    39	   (secrets.nix, `$name-cert-alt-names` comparison), so deployed nodes pick
    40	   the new SAN up on the next deploy — but anything that pinned the old cert
    41	   must tolerate rotation.
    42	
    43	3. **Macaroon creation runs in lnd's `ExecStartPost`** (modules/lnd.nix): a
    44	   root script curls `https://<nbLib.address restAddress>:<restPort>/v1` with
    45	   `--cacert`. It would move from 127.0.0.1 to 10.10.0.1 → fails without
    46	   item 2 and item 1 → **lnd.service itself fails post-start**, even though
    47	   the daemon is healthy.
    48	
    49	4. **btcpayserver's lnd-rest backend** (modules/btcpayserver.nix,
    50	   `btclightning=...server=https://<address restAddress>...`) moves to
    51	   10.10.0.1 too: needs the SAN fix and makes btcpayserver transitively
    52	   wg-dependent.
    53	
    54	5. **lndconnect onion mode** (modules/lndconnect.nix): the Tor hidden
    55	   service forwards to `nbLib.address lnd.restAddress`. Tor-based mobile
    56	   access would then depend on the wg interface being up.
    57	
    58	6. **nodeinfo** (modules/nodeinfo.nix) reports `rest_address` — cosmetic,
    59	   but scripts parsing it see a new value.
    60	
    61	## Recommended design (avoids 2–6 entirely)
    62	
    63	Do **not** touch `restAddress`. Leave it at its default `127.0.0.1` and have
    64	the preset add a *second* listener — lnd accepts repeated `restlisten`
    65	entries:
    66	
    67	```nix
    68	services.lnd.extraConfig = ''
    69	  restlisten=${serverAddress}:${toString lnd.restPort}
    70	'';
    71	```
    72	
    73	All internal consumers keep localhost (valid cert SAN, no wg coupling); the
    74	wg peer reaches `10.10.0.1:8080` exactly as before (`lndconnect-wg` already
    75	targets `serverAddress` with `--nocert`). Only item 1 — the bind-time
    76	dependency on the interface — remains, and still needs:
    77	
    78	- `systemd.services.lnd = { wants = [ "wireguard-wg-nb.service" ]; after = [ "wireguard-wg-nb.service" ]; }`
    79	  in the preset (runtime wg restarts are fine: a bound socket survives
    80	  interface teardown and resumes when the address returns);
    81	- the firewall accept rule stays (nixos-fw default-drops; the rule is what
    82	  admits the peer). Since 2026-09-09 it is already scoped to the interface
    83	  and the single peer (`-i wg-nb -s <peerAddress>/32`), closing the
    84	  2026-08-29 low: a spoofed-source packet on the external interface no
    85	  longer matches the rule at all, independent of rp_filter/routing.
    86	
    87	## Pre-flight checklist before shipping any variant
    88	
    89	- [ ] Decide on the lnd↔wg startup coupling (accept, or `ip_nonlocal_bind`).
    90	- [ ] `test/wireguard-lndconnect.nix`: assert lnd comes up cleanly from a
    91	      cold boot (ordering race), REST reachable over wg at
    92	      `serverAddress:restPort`, REST **not** bound on the external
    93	      interface (the actual point of the fix), macaroon `ExecStartPost`
    94	      succeeded, and — if btcpayserver is in the scenario — its lnd backend
    95	      connects.
    96	- [ ] Full local VM suite green (`./test/run-tests.sh`).
    97	- [ ] Verify on a staging deploy that cert regeneration (if `restAddress`
    98	      was changed rather than the extraConfig variant) does not strand
    99	      existing clients.
   100	- [ ] Re-run the adversarial LLM audit (audits branch) — per policy every
   101	      fix re-runs the whole review; the review should specifically re-check
   102	      items 1–6.

    68	      # nix-bitcoin-release.nix. Never fetches upstream fort-nix releases
    69	      # (they lack this fork's fixes). Tags are unsigned CI pointers — the
    70	      # commit is what you review; see SECURITY.md.
    71	      set -euo pipefail

    73	      repo=https://github.com/ostermayer/nix-bitcoin

    75	      [[ $tag ]] || { echo "No release tag found on $repo" >&2; exit 1; }

    77	      [[ $commit ]] || commit=$(git ls-remote "$repo" "refs/tags/$tag" | cut -f1)

    82	      if [[ $new == "$current" ]]; then

    84	      else
    85	        printf '%s' "$new" > "$releaseFile"
    86	        echo "Pinned $tag ($commit) in nix-bitcoin-release.nix."
    87	        echo "Review that commit first — the next nix-shell evaluates the new tree's shellHook:"
    88	        echo "  https://github.com/ostermayer/nix-bitcoin/compare/$(echo "$current" | sed -n 's|.*/archive/\([0-9a-f]*\)\.tar\.gz.*|\1|p')...$commit"

    12	/* Netns names of the services this fork ships (see modules/netns-isolation.nix) */
    13	static char *allowed_netns[] = {
    14	    "nb-bitcoind",
    15	    "nb-lnd",
    16	    "nb-electrs",
    17	    "nb-nbxplorer",
    18	    "nb-btcpayserver"
    19	};
    20	
    21	int is_netns_allowed(char *netns) {
    22	    int n_allowed_netns = sizeof(allowed_netns) / sizeof(allowed_netns[0]);
    23	    for (int i = 0; i < n_allowed_netns; i++) {
    24	        if (strcmp(allowed_netns[i], netns) == 0) {
    25	            return 1;
    26	        }
    27	    }
    28	    return 0;
    29	}
    30	
    31	void print_capabilities() {
    32	    cap_t caps = cap_get_proc();
    33	    printf("Capabilities: %s\n", cap_to_text(caps, NULL));
    34	    cap_free(caps);
    35	}
    36	
    37	int drop_capabilities() {
    38	    cap_t caps = cap_get_proc();
    39	    if (caps == NULL) return -1;
    40	    if (cap_clear(caps) < 0) { cap_free(caps); return -1; }
    41	    int rc = cap_set_proc(caps);
    42	    cap_free(caps);
    43	    return rc;
    44	}
    45	
    46	int main(int argc, char **argv) {
    47	    char netns_path[256];
    48	
    49	    if (argc < 3) {
    50	        printf("usage: %s <netns> <command>\n", argv[0]);
    51	        return 1;
    52	    }
    53	
    54	    if (!is_netns_allowed(argv[1])) {
    55	        printf("%s is not an allowed netns.\n", argv[1]);
    56	        return 1;
    57	    }
    58	
    59	    /* Require an absolute path so PATH cannot redirect the exec target.
    60	       The caller's environment is otherwise inherited; the operator who may
    61	       run this wrapper is already root-equivalent. */
    62	    if (argv[2][0] != '/') {
    63	        printf("command must be an absolute path.\n");
    64	        return 1;
    65	    }
    66	
    67	    int len = snprintf(netns_path, sizeof(netns_path), "/var/run/netns/%s", argv[1]);
    68	    if (len < 0 || (size_t)len >= sizeof(netns_path)) {
    69	        printf("Path length exceeded for netns %s.\n", argv[1]);
    70	        return 1;
    71	    }
    72	
    73	    int fd = open(netns_path, O_RDONLY);
    74	    if (fd < 0) {
    75	        printf("Failed opening netns %s: %d, %s \n", netns_path, errno, strerror(errno));
    76	        return 1;
    77	    }
    78	
    79	    if (setns(fd, CLONE_NEWNET) < 0) {
    80	        printf("Failed setns %d, %s \n", errno, strerror(errno));
    81	        close(fd);
    82	        return 1;
    83	    }
    84	    close(fd);
    85	
    86	    /* Drop capabilities */
    87	    #ifdef DEBUG
    88	    print_capabilities();
    89	    #endif
    90	    if (drop_capabilities() < 0) {
    91	        printf("Failed to drop capabilities: %d, %s \n", errno, strerror(errno));
    92	        return 1;
    93	    }
    94	    #ifdef DEBUG
    95	    print_capabilities();
    96	    #endif
    97	
    98	    execv(argv[2], &argv[2]);
    99	    /* Only reached on exec failure */
   100	    printf("Failed to exec %s: %d, %s \n", argv[2], errno, strerror(errno));
   101	    return 1;
   102	}

     4	# Private/public keys are created via the secrets system.
     5	# Add helper binaries `nix-bitcoin-wg-connect` and optionally `lndconnect-wg`.
     6	
     7	# See ../../docs/services.md ("Use Zeus (mobile lightning wallet) via WireGuard")
     8	# for usage instructions.
     9	
    10	# This is a rather opinionated implementation that lacks the flexibility offered by
    11	# other nix-bitcoin modules, so ship this as a `preset`.
    12	# Some users will prefer to use `lndconnect` with their existing WireGuard or Tailscale setup.
    13

    21	    };
    22	    restrictPeer = mkOption {
    23	      type = types.bool;

    49	  # mobile app.
    50	  wgConnect = pkgs.writers.writeBashBin "nix-bitcoin-wg-connect" ''
    51	    set -euo pipefail
    52	    text=
    53	    host=
    54	    for arg in "$@"; do
    55	      case $arg in
    56	        --text)
    57	          text=1
    58	          ;;
    59	        *)
    60	          host=$arg
    61	          ;;
    62	      esac
    63	    done
    64	
    65	    if [[ ! $host ]]; then
    66	      # Use lndconnect to fetch the external ip.
    67	      # This internally uses https://github.com/GlenDC/go-external-ip, which
    68	      # queries a set of external ip providers.
    69	      host=$(
    70	        ${getExe config.nix-bitcoin.pkgs.lndconnect} --url --nocert \
    71	          --configfile=/dev/null --adminmacaroonpath=/dev/null \
    72	          | sed -nE 's|.*?/(.*?):.*|\1|p'
    73	      )
    74	    fi
    75	
    76	    config="[Interface]
    77	    PrivateKey = $(cat ${secretsDir}/wg-peer-private-key)
    78	    Address = ${peerAddress}/24
    79	
    80	    [Peer]
    81	    PublicKey = $(cat ${secretsDir}/wg-server-public-key)
    82	    AllowedIPs = ${wgSubnet}.0/24
    83	    Endpoint = $host:${toString wg-nb.listenPort}
    84	    PersistentKeepalive = 25
    85	    "
    86	
    87	    if [[ $text ]]; then

    89	    else
    90	      echo "$config" | ${getExe pkgs.qrencode} -t UTF8 -o -
    91	    fi
    92	  '';
    93	in {

    95	
    96	  config = {
    97	    assertions = [

    99	        # Don't support `netns-isolation` for now to keep things simple
   100	        assertion = !(config.nix-bitcoin.netns-isolation.enable or false);
   101	        message = "`nix-bitcoin.wireguard` is not compatible with `netns-isolation`.";
   102	      }
   103	      # The whole security model of this preset is the iptables rule set
   104	      # below: the lnd admin REST listener is bound to 0.0.0.0 (see the comment
   105	      # at `restAddress`) and the peer restriction is an iptables REJECT. Two
   106	      # silent ways to lose that (audit 2026-09-09): the firewall disabled, or
   107	      # the nftables backend enabled — NixOS then ignores `extraCommands`
   108	      # without an error, so the REST accept rule and the restrictPeer REJECT
   109	      # simply vanish. Refuse to evaluate rather than ship an unguarded admin API.
   110	      {

   117	      }

   120	    networking.wireguard.interfaces.wg-nb = {

   122	      listenPort = mkDefault 51820;

   124	      allowedIPsAsRoutes = false;

   128	          # `peer0` and replace it via `getPubkeyFromFile` (see further below)
   129	          # at peer service runtime.

   137	      wireguard-wg-nb = rec {
   138	        wants = [ "nix-bitcoin-secrets.target" ];
   139	        after = wants;
   140	      };
   141	
   142	      # Read the peer pubkey from the secrets file at service runtime.
   143	      # (Upstream patched its own source with sed and re-executed it as root,
   144	      # which turned a secrets-store write into root RCE; audit L-8.)
   145	      wireguard-wg-nb-peer-peer0 = {
   146	        script = mkForce ''
   147	          ${pkgs.wireguard-tools}/bin/wg set wg-nb peer "$(cat "${secretsDir}/wg-peer-public-key")" allowed-ips ${peerAddress}/32
   148	        '';
   149	        postStop = mkForce ''
   150	          ${pkgs.wireguard-tools}/bin/wg set wg-nb peer "$(cat "${secretsDir}/wg-peer-public-key")" remove || true
   151	        '';
   152	      };

   156	      wgConnect

   159	        exec lndconnect --host "${serverAddress}" --nocert "$@"
   160	      '')
   161	    );
   162	
   163	    networking.firewall = let
   164	      restrictPeerRule = "-s ${peerAddress} ! -d ${serverAddress} -j REJECT";
   165	    in {
   166	      allowedUDPPorts = [ wg-nb.listenPort ];
   167	
   168	      extraCommands =
   169	        optionalString lndconnect ''
   170	          # Admit lnd REST only from the single WireGuard peer, and only when the
   171	          # packet actually arrived over the wg-nb interface. `-s` alone matches
   172	          # a spoofed source on any interface; `-i` pins it to the tunnel (audit
   173	          # 2026-08-29, low). This is the interim hardening while the
   174	          # 0.0.0.0 bind itself stays deferred (see docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md).
   175	          iptables -w -A nixos-fw -i wg-nb -p tcp -s ${peerAddress}/32 --dport ${toString lnd.restPort} -j nixos-fw-accept
   176	        ''
   177	        + optionalString cfg.restrictPeer ''
   178	          # Insert at the top: appended (-A) the REJECT would sit behind the
   179	          # standard accept rules (allowed ports, the REST accept above), so a
   180	          # peer could still reach any firewall-allowed port on the node's
   181	          # other addresses through the tunnel (second opinion 2026-09-09).
   182	          # Replies to server-initiated traffic are unaffected: their
   183	          # destination is serverAddress.
   184	          iptables -w -I nixos-fw 1 ${restrictPeerRule}
   185	          iptables -w -I FORWARD 1 ${restrictPeerRule}
   186	        '';
   187	
   188	      extraStopCommands =
   189	        # Rules added to chain `nixos-fw` are automatically removed when restarting
   190	        # the NixOS firewall service.
   191	        mkIf cfg.restrictPeer ''
   192	          iptables -w -D FORWARD ${restrictPeerRule} || :
   193	        '';
   194	    };
   195	
   196	    # Listen on all addresses, including `serverAddress`.
   197	    # The listen port is guarded by the firewall (nixos-fw default-drops; only
   198	    # the wg subnet accept rule above admits REST traffic), but this is still
   199	    # a known audit finding (2026-08-24, advisory): the admin REST API relies
   200	    # on the firewall alone. Binding to `serverAddress` instead is DEFERRED —
   201	    # `nbLib.address` maps 0.0.0.0 to 127.0.0.1, so every internal consumer
   202	    # of `restAddress` (macaroon ExecStartPost, btcpayserver, lndconnect
   203	    # onion target) silently depends on this value, and a naive rebind breaks
   204	    # them plus lnd startup ordering. Read docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md and
   205	    # work through its checklist before changing this line.
   206	    services.lnd = mkIf lndconnect {
   207	      restAddress = "0.0.0.0";
   208	      tor.enforce = false;
   209	    };
   210	
   211	    nix-bitcoin.secrets = {
   212	      wg-server-private-key = {};
   213	      wg-server-public-key = { user = wgConnectUser; group = "root"; };
   214	      wg-peer-private-key = { user = wgConnectUser; group = "root"; };
   215	      wg-peer-public-key = {};
   216	    };
   217	
   218	    nix-bitcoin.generateSecretsCmds.wireguard = let
   219	      wg = "${pkgs.wireguard-tools}/bin/wg";
   220	    in ''
   221	      makeWireguardKey() {
   222	        local name=$1
   223	        local priv=wg-$name-private-key
   224	        local pub=wg-$name-public-key
   225	        if [[ ! -e $priv ]]; then
   226	          ${wg} genkey > $priv
   227	        fi
   228	        if [[ $priv -nt $pub ]]; then
   229	          ${wg} pubkey < $priv > $pub
   230	        fi
   231	      }
   232	      makeWireguardKey server
   233	      makeWireguardKey peer
   234	    '';
   235	  };
   236	}
     1	# Deferred: bind lnd's REST API to the WireGuard address
     2	
     3	Status: **DEFERRED — do not implement without working through every item below.**
     4	
     5	The 2026-08-24 adversarial audit (run `2026-08-24-111031`, both models) flagged
     6	that the wireguard preset sets `services.lnd.restAddress = "0.0.0.0"`
     7	(modules/presets/wireguard.nix), exposing the macaroon-admin REST API on all
     8	interfaces and relying solely on the nixos-fw firewall (default-drop plus an
     9	accept rule for the wg subnet) for protection. Severity medium/low, advisory.
    10	The suggested fix — bind to the wg server address (`${subnet}.1`, default
    11	10.10.0.1) instead — is correct as defense-in-depth but **breaks several
    12	things that are non-obvious**, because of one load-bearing detail:
    13	
    14	> `nbLib.address` (pkgs/lib.nix) maps the bind address `0.0.0.0` to the
    15	> *connect* address `127.0.0.1`. Every internal consumer of `restAddress`
    16	> goes through it. Today they all silently talk to localhost, which the
    17	> default TLS cert covers. Bind to `10.10.0.1` and they **all** follow.
    18	
    19	## What a naive `restAddress = serverAddress` breaks
    20	
    21	1. **lnd startup becomes hard-coupled to WireGuard.** lnd fails fatally if it
    22	   cannot bind `restlisten`; the `10.10.0.1` address only exists once
    23	   `wireguard-wg-nb.service` has configured the interface. Without systemd
    24	   ordering, boot is a crash-loop race (`Restart=on-failure`/10s masks it
    25	   until it doesn't). *With* ordering, a broken wg setup (e.g. missing
    26	   `wg-server-private-key` secret) takes the whole lightning node down —
    27	   today lnd runs fine with wg broken. This coupling is inherent to the fix;
    28	   it must be a conscious acceptance, or defused with
    29	   `net.ipv4.ip_nonlocal_bind = 1` (system-wide sysctl loosening — an
    30	   auditor trade-off of its own).
    31	
    32	2. **TLS cert SANs.** The generated lnd cert only has
    33	   `DNS:localhost,IP:127.0.0.1` plus `rpcAddress` when non-local
    34	   (modules/secrets/secrets.nix `doMakeCert`; modules/lnd.nix
    35	   `certificate.extraIPs`). Nothing adds the REST address. Any validating
    36	   client connecting to `https://10.10.0.1:8080` fails TLS. The preset must
    37	   set `services.lnd.certificate.extraIPs = [ serverAddress ]`. Note
    38	   `makeCert` *does* regenerate the cert when alt-names change
    39	   (secrets.nix, `$name-cert-alt-names` comparison), so deployed nodes pick
    40	   the new SAN up on the next deploy — but anything that pinned the old cert
    41	   must tolerate rotation.
    42	
    43	3. **Macaroon creation runs in lnd's `ExecStartPost`** (modules/lnd.nix): a
    44	   root script curls `https://<nbLib.address restAddress>:<restPort>/v1` with
    45	   `--cacert`. It would move from 127.0.0.1 to 10.10.0.1 → fails without
    46	   item 2 and item 1 → **lnd.service itself fails post-start**, even though
    47	   the daemon is healthy.
    48	
    49	4. **btcpayserver's lnd-rest backend** (modules/btcpayserver.nix,
    50	   `btclightning=...server=https://<address restAddress>...`) moves to
    51	   10.10.0.1 too: needs the SAN fix and makes btcpayserver transitively
    52	   wg-dependent.
    53	
    54	5. **lndconnect onion mode** (modules/lndconnect.nix): the Tor hidden
    55	   service forwards to `nbLib.address lnd.restAddress`. Tor-based mobile
    56	   access would then depend on the wg interface being up.
    57	
    58	6. **nodeinfo** (modules/nodeinfo.nix) reports `rest_address` — cosmetic,
    59	   but scripts parsing it see a new value.
    60	
    61	## Recommended design (avoids 2–6 entirely)
    62	
    63	Do **not** touch `restAddress`. Leave it at its default `127.0.0.1` and have
    64	the preset add a *second* listener — lnd accepts repeated `restlisten`
    65	entries:
    66	
    67	```nix
    68	services.lnd.extraConfig = ''
    69	  restlisten=${serverAddress}:${toString lnd.restPort}
    70	'';
    71	```
    72	
    73	All internal consumers keep localhost (valid cert SAN, no wg coupling); the
    74	wg peer reaches `10.10.0.1:8080` exactly as before (`lndconnect-wg` already
    75	targets `serverAddress` with `--nocert`). Only item 1 — the bind-time
    76	dependency on the interface — remains, and still needs:
    77	
    78	- `systemd.services.lnd = { wants = [ "wireguard-wg-nb.service" ]; after = [ "wireguard-wg-nb.service" ]; }`
    79	  in the preset (runtime wg restarts are fine: a bound socket survives
    80	  interface teardown and resumes when the address returns);
    81	- the firewall accept rule stays (nixos-fw default-drops; the rule is what
    82	  admits the peer). Since 2026-09-09 it is already scoped to the interface
    83	  and the single peer (`-i wg-nb -s <peerAddress>/32`), closing the
    84	  2026-08-29 low: a spoofed-source packet on the external interface no
    85	  longer matches the rule at all, independent of rp_filter/routing.
    86	
    87	## Pre-flight checklist before shipping any variant
    88	
    89	- [ ] Decide on the lnd↔wg startup coupling (accept, or `ip_nonlocal_bind`).
    90	- [ ] `test/wireguard-lndconnect.nix`: assert lnd comes up cleanly from a
    91	      cold boot (ordering race), REST reachable over wg at
    92	      `serverAddress:restPort`, REST **not** bound on the external
    93	      interface (the actual point of the fix), macaroon `ExecStartPost`
    94	      succeeded, and — if btcpayserver is in the scenario — its lnd backend
    95	      connects.
    96	- [ ] Full local VM suite green (`./test/run-tests.sh`).
    97	- [ ] Verify on a staging deploy that cert regeneration (if `restAddress`
    98	      was changed rather than the extraConfig variant) does not strand
    99	      existing clients.
   100	- [ ] Re-run the adversarial LLM audit (audits branch) — per policy every
   101	      fix re-runs the whole review; the review should specifically re-check
   102	      items 1–6.

    65	    update-nix-bitcoin() {(
    66	      # Fork-native pin: resolve the fork's latest CalVer release tag to the
    67	      # commit it points at, prefetch that commit's tarball and rewrite
    68	      # nix-bitcoin-release.nix. Never fetches upstream fort-nix releases
    69	      # (they lack this fork's fixes). Tags are unsigned CI pointers — the
    70	      # commit is what you review; see SECURITY.md.
    71	      set -euo pipefail
    72	      releaseFile="${cfgDir}/nix-bitcoin-release.nix"
    73	      repo=https://github.com/ostermayer/nix-bitcoin
    74	      tag=$(git ls-remote --tags --refs "$repo" 'refs/tags/20*' | sed 's|.*refs/tags/||' | sort -V | tail -n1)
    75	      [[ $tag ]] || { echo "No release tag found on $repo" >&2; exit 1; }
    76	      commit=$(git ls-remote "$repo" "refs/tags/$tag^{}" | cut -f1)
    77	      [[ $commit ]] || commit=$(git ls-remote "$repo" "refs/tags/$tag" | cut -f1)
    78	      url="$repo/archive/$commit.tar.gz"
    79	      hash=$(nix-prefetch-url --unpack "$url" 2>/dev/null)
    80	      new=$(printf 'builtins.fetchTarball {\n  # fork release %s\n  url = "%s";\n  sha256 = "%s";\n}\n' "$tag" "$url" "$hash")
    81	      current=$(cat "$releaseFile" 2>/dev/null || true)
    82	      if [[ $new == "$current" ]]; then
    83	        echo "nix-bitcoin-release.nix already pins $tag ($commit)"
    84	      else
    85	        printf '%s' "$new" > "$releaseFile"
    86	        echo "Pinned $tag ($commit) in nix-bitcoin-release.nix."
    87	        echo "Review that commit first — the next nix-shell evaluates the new tree's shellHook:"
    88	        echo "  https://github.com/ostermayer/nix-bitcoin/compare/$(echo "$current" | sed -n 's|.*/archive/\([0-9a-f]*\)\.tar\.gz.*|\1|p')...$commit"
    89	        echo "then re-enter the deployment shell with: exit; nix-shell"
    90	        # Deliberately no automatic `exec nix-shell`: that would run the newly
    91	        # pinned revision's shell code on this machine before the review the
    92	        # line above asks for (second opinion 2026-09-09).
    93	      fi
    94	    )}
    95	
    96	    generate-secrets() {(
    97	      set -euo pipefail
    98	      config="${cfgDir}/krops/krops-configuration.nix"
    99	      if [[ ! -e $config ]]; then
   100	        config="${cfgDir}/configuration.nix"
   101	      fi
   102	      genSecrets=$(nix-build --no-out-link -I nixos-config="$config" \
   103	                   '<nixpkgs/nixos>' -A config.nix-bitcoin.generateSecretsScript)
   104	      mkdir -p "${cfgDir}/secrets"
   105	      (cd "${cfgDir}/secrets"; $genSecrets)
   106	    )}
   107	
   108	    deploy() {(
   109	      set -euo pipefail
   110	      krops-deploy &
   111	      kropsPid=$!
   112	      if eval-config; then
   113	        wait $kropsPid
   114	      else
   115	        # Kill all subprocesses
   116	        kill $(pidClosure $kropsPid)
   117	        return 1
   118	      fi
   119	    )}
   120	
   121	    krops-deploy() {(
   122	      set -euo pipefail
   123	      generate-secrets
   124	      # Ensure strict permissions on secrets/ directory before rsyncing it to
   125	      # the target machine
   126	      chmod 700 "${cfgDir}/secrets"
   127	      $(nix-build --no-out-link "${cfgDir}/krops/deploy.nix")
   128	    )}
   129	
   130	    eval-config() {(
   131	      set -euo pipefail
   132	      system=$(getNodeSystem)
   133	      NIXOS_CONFIG="${cfgDir}/krops/krops-configuration.nix" \
   134	        nix-instantiate --eval ${nixpkgs}/nixos $system -A system.outPath | tr -d '"'
   135	      echo
   136	    )}
   137	
   138	    build-config() {(
   139	      set -euo pipefail
   140	      system=$(getNodeSystem)
   141	      NIXOS_CONFIG="${cfgDir}/krops/krops-configuration.nix" \
   142	        nix-build --no-out-link ${nixpkgs}/nixos $system -A system
   143	    )}
   144	
   145	    getNodeSystem() {
   146	      if [[ -e '${cfgDir}/krops/system' ]]; then
   147	        echo -n "--argstr system "; cat '${cfgDir}/krops/system'
   148	      elif [[ $OSTYPE == darwin* ]]; then
   149	        # On macOS, `builtins.currentSystem` (`*-darwin`) can never equal
   150	        # the node system (`*-linux`), so we can always provide a helpful error message:
   151	        >&2 echo "Error, node system not set. See here how to fix this:"
   152	        >&2 echo "https://github.com/fort-nix/nix-bitcoin/blob/master/docs/install.md#optional-specify-the-system-of-your-node"
   153	        return 1
   154	      fi
   155	    };
   156	
   157	    pidClosure() {
   158	      echo "$1"
   159	      for pid in $(ps -o pid= --ppid "$1"); do
   160	        pidClosure "$pid"
   161	      done
   162	    }
   163	
   164	    if [[ $isInteractive ]]; then
   165	      ${pkgs.figlet}/bin/figlet "nix-bitcoin"
   166	      echo 'Enter "h" or "help" for documentation.'
   167	    fi
   168	
   169	    # Don't run this hook when another nix-shell is run inside this shell
   170	    unset shellHook
   171	
   172	    ${extraShellInitCmds pkgs}
   173	  '';
   174	}

     3	#define _GNU_SOURCE
     4	#include <sched.h>
     5	#include <stdio.h>

    12	/* Netns names of the services this fork ships (see modules/netns-isolation.nix) */
    13	static char *allowed_netns[] = {
    14	    "nb-bitcoind",
    15	    "nb-lnd",
    16	    "nb-electrs",
    17	    "nb-nbxplorer",
    18	    "nb-btcpayserver"
    19	};
    20	
    21	int is_netns_allowed(char *netns) {
    22	    int n_allowed_netns = sizeof(allowed_netns) / sizeof(allowed_netns[0]);
    23	    for (int i = 0; i < n_allowed_netns; i++) {
    24	        if (strcmp(allowed_netns[i], netns) == 0) {
    25	            return 1;
    26	        }
    27	    }
    28	    return 0;
    29	}
    30	
    31	void print_capabilities() {
    32	    cap_t caps = cap_get_proc();
    33	    printf("Capabilities: %s\n", cap_to_text(caps, NULL));
    34	    cap_free(caps);
    35	}
    36	
    37	int drop_capabilities() {
    38	    cap_t caps = cap_get_proc();
    39	    if (caps == NULL) return -1;
    40	    if (cap_clear(caps) < 0) { cap_free(caps); return -1; }
    41	    int rc = cap_set_proc(caps);
    42	    cap_free(caps);
    43	    return rc;
    44	}
    45	
    46	int main(int argc, char **argv) {
    47	    char netns_path[256];
    48	
    49	    if (argc < 3) {
    50	        printf("usage: %s <netns> <command>\n", argv[0]);
    51	        return 1;
    52	    }
    53	
    54	    if (!is_netns_allowed(argv[1])) {
    55	        printf("%s is not an allowed netns.\n", argv[1]);
    56	        return 1;
    57	    }
    58	
    59	    /* Require an absolute path so PATH cannot redirect the exec target.
    60	       The caller's environment is otherwise inherited; the operator who may
    61	       run this wrapper is already root-equivalent. */
    62	    if (argv[2][0] != '/') {
    63	        printf("command must be an absolute path.\n");
    64	        return 1;
    65	    }
    66	
    67	    int len = snprintf(netns_path, sizeof(netns_path), "/var/run/netns/%s", argv[1]);
    68	    if (len < 0 || (size_t)len >= sizeof(netns_path)) {
    69	        printf("Path length exceeded for netns %s.\n", argv[1]);
    70	        return 1;
    71	    }
    72	
    73	    int fd = open(netns_path, O_RDONLY);
    74	    if (fd < 0) {
    75	        printf("Failed opening netns %s: %d, %s \n", netns_path, errno, strerror(errno));
    76	        return 1;
    77	    }
    78	
    79	    if (setns(fd, CLONE_NEWNET) < 0) {
    80	        printf("Failed setns %d, %s \n", errno, strerror(errno));
    81	        close(fd);
    82	        return 1;
    83	    }
    84	    close(fd);
    85	
    86	    /* Drop capabilities */
    87	    #ifdef DEBUG
    88	    print_capabilities();
    89	    #endif
    90	    if (drop_capabilities() < 0) {
    91	        printf("Failed to drop capabilities: %d, %s \n", errno, strerror(errno));
    92	        return 1;
    93	    }
    94	    #ifdef DEBUG
    95	    print_capabilities();
    96	    #endif
    97	
    98	    execv(argv[2], &argv[2]);
    99	    /* Only reached on exec failure */
   100	    printf("Failed to exec %s: %d, %s \n", argv[2], errno, strerror(errno));
   101	    return 1;
   102	}

     1	# RPC calls that are safe for public use.
     2	#
     3	# Safety bar: bounded CPU cost per call, no P2P side effects beyond
     4	# transaction relay, no leaks of peer topology or wallet contents.
     5	#
     6	# Deliberately excluded (audit M-3):
     7	# - scantxoutset, gettxoutsetinfo, getblocktemplate: minutes-scale CPU,
     8	#   trivially abused for DoS by any holder of the public credentials.
     9	# - getblockfrompeer: lets the caller drive the node's P2P block fetching.
    10	# - fundrawtransaction: operates on a loaded bitcoind wallet — it selects and
    11	#   locks UTXOs and reveals change/wallet contents, violating the wallet-safety
    12	#   bar above. No local consumer (lnd, electrs, nbxplorer) uses it; lnd/nbxplorer
    13	#   manage their own wallets and electrs only reads blocks. Removed in the audit
    14	#   follow-up 2026-08-20.
    15	# If you need any of these, grant them via
    16	# `services.bitcoind.rpc.users.public.rpcwhitelist` in your own config.
    17	#
    18	# NOTE: getpeerinfo / getnodeaddresses stay in the list. This "public" user
    19	# is also the default RPC user that LOCAL services use, and lnd REQUIRES both
    20	# (its chain-backend health check + pruned-mode block fetching) — removing
    21	# them makes lnd fail its health check and shut itself down. They only leak
    22	# peer topology if you actually expose this user over a public proxy; if you
    23	# do that, narrow the whitelist in your own config rather than here.

    36	      SystemCallFilter = [ "@system-service" "~add_key kcmp keyctl mbind move_pages name_to_handle_at personality process_vm_readv process_vm_writev request_key setns unshare userfaultfd" ];

     4	with pkgs.lib;
     5	
     6	makeTestVM {
     7	  name = "wireguard-lndconnect";
     8	
     9	  nodes = {
    10	    server = {
    11	      imports = [
    12	        ../modules/modules.nix
    13	        ../modules/presets/wireguard.nix
    14	      ];
    15	
    16	      nixpkgs.pkgs = pkgs;
    17	
    18	      nix-bitcoin.generateSecrets = true;
    19	      nix-bitcoin.operator.enable = true;
    20	
    21	      services.lnd = {
    22	        enable = true;
    23	        lndconnect.enable = true;
    24	        port = 9736;
    25	      };
    26	    };
    27	
    28	    client = {
    29	      nixpkgs.pkgs = pkgs;
    30	
    31	      environment.systemPackages = with pkgs; [
    32	        wireguard-tools
    33	      ];
    34	    };
    35	  };
    36	
    37	  testScript =  ''
    38	    import base64
    39	    import urllib.parse as Url
    40	    from types import SimpleNamespace
    41	
    42	    def parse_lndconnect_url(url):
    43	        u = Url.urlparse(url)
    44	        data = {'host': u.hostname, 'port': u.port}
    45	        queries = Url.parse_qs(u.query)
    46	        macaroon = queries['macaroon'][0]
    47	        data['macaroon_hex'] = base64.urlsafe_b64decode(macaroon + '===').hex().upper()
    48	        return SimpleNamespace(**data)
    49	
    50	    client.start()
    51	    server.connect()
    52	
    53	    if not "is_interactive" in vars():
    54	
    55	      with subtest("connect client to server via WireGuard"):
    56	          server.wait_for_unit("wireguard-wg-nb-peer-peer0.service")
    57	
    58	          # Get WireGuard config from server and save it to `/tmp/wireguard.conf` on the client
    59	          wg_config = server.succeed("runuser -u operator -- nix-bitcoin-wg-connect server --text")
    60	          # Encode to base64
    61	          b64 = base64.b64encode(wg_config.encode('utf-8')).decode()
    62	          client.succeed(f"install -m 400 <(echo -n {b64} | base64 -d) /tmp/wireguard.conf")
    63	
    64	          # Connect to server via WireGuard
    65	          client.succeed("wg-quick up /tmp/wireguard.conf")
    66	
    67	          # Ping server from client
    68	          print(client.succeed("ping -c 1 -W 0.5 10.10.0.1"))
    69	
    70	      with subtest("lndconnect-wg"):
    71	          server.wait_for_unit("lnd.service")
    72	          lndconnect_url = server.succeed("runuser -u operator -- lndconnect-wg --url")
    73	          api = parse_lndconnect_url(lndconnect_url)
    74	          # Make lnd REST API call
    75	          client.succeed(
    76	              f"curl -fsS --max-time 3 --insecure --header 'Grpc-Metadata-macaroon: {api.macaroon_hex}' "
    77	              f"-X GET https://{api.host}:{api.port}/v1/getinfo"
    78	          )
    79	  '';
    80	}
     1	name: Release tag
     2	
     3	# Every push to `release` is a fleet-validated snapshot (the branch ruleset only
     4	# accepts commits with all CI checks green). Tag those snapshots so advisories,
     5	# users, and downstream pins can name a version instead of a bare commit hash.
     6	#
     7	# CalVer (YYYY.MM.DD[.N]): for a security-sensitive fork, "how fresh is this
     8	# pin" is the question a version should answer, and it never collides with
     9	# upstream's frozen v0.0.x line. Only closure-affecting pushes are tagged;
    10	# CI/docs-only fast-forwards are skipped.
    11	on:
    12	  push:
    13	    branches: [release]
    14	
    15	permissions:
    16	  contents: write
    17	
    18	concurrency:
    19	  group: release-tag
    20	  cancel-in-progress: false
    21	
    22	jobs:
    23	  tag:
    24	    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    25	    steps:
    26	      - uses: actions/checkout@11d5960a326750d5838078e36cf38b85af677262 # v4
    27	        with:
    28	          fetch-depth: 0
    29	      - name: Tag and release if the closure changed
    30	        env:
    31	          GH_TOKEN: ${{ github.token }}
    32	        run: |
    33	          set -euo pipefail
    34	          git config user.name  "github-actions[bot]"
    35	          git config user.email "41898282+github-actions[bot]@users.noreply.github.com"
    36	          prev=$(git describe --tags --abbrev=0 --match '20*' 2>/dev/null || echo "")
    37	
    38	          # Skip when nothing closure-affecting changed since the last release.
    39	          if [ -n "$prev" ]; then
    40	            if git diff --quiet "$prev"..HEAD -- modules pkgs overlay.nix default.nix flake.nix flake.lock; then
    41	              echo "No closure-affecting change since $prev — not tagging."
    42	              exit 0
    43	            fi
    44	          fi
    45	
    46	          date=$(date -u +%Y.%m.%d)
    47	          tag=$date; n=1
    48	          while git rev-parse -q --verify "refs/tags/$tag" >/dev/null; do
    49	            n=$((n+1)); tag="$date.$n"
    50	          done
    51	
    52	          {
    53	            echo "Fleet-validated snapshot of the maintained nix-bitcoin fork."
    54	            echo
    55	            echo "## nixpkgs pins"
    56	            for inp in nixpkgs nixpkgs-unstable; do
    57	              rev=$(jq -r ".nodes.\"$inp\".locked.rev // empty" flake.lock)
    58	              [ -n "$rev" ] && echo "- \`$inp\`: $rev"
    59	            done
    60	            echo
    61	            echo "## Changes since ${prev:-the beginning}"
    62	            if [ -n "$prev" ]; then git log --no-merges --pretty='- %s' "$prev"..HEAD; else echo "- initial tagged release"; fi
    63	          } > notes.md
    64	
    65	          git tag -a "$tag" -m "Release $tag"
    66	          git push origin "refs/tags/$tag"
    67	          gh release create "$tag" --title "$tag" --notes-file notes.md
    68	          echo "Released $tag"
     1	#!/usr/bin/env nix-shell
     2	#!nix-shell -i bash -p bash coreutils curl jq gnupg gnugrep
     3	set -euo pipefail
     4	
     5	scriptDir=$(cd "${BASH_SOURCE[0]%/*}" && pwd)
     6	
     7	trap 'echo "Error at ${BASH_SOURCE[0]}:$LINENO"' ERR
     8	
     9	repo=fort-nix/nix-bitcoin
    10	
    11	# This legacy helper fetches an UPSTREAM (fort-nix) signed release tarball. This
    12	# repository is a maintained FORK; upstream's release artifacts do NOT contain
    13	# this fork's fixes, so fetching them here would silently ship un-fixed code.
    14	# Consume this fork via its flake pin instead:
    15	#   inputs.nix-bitcoin.url = "github:ostermayer/nix-bitcoin/release";
    16	# Refuse to run unless explicitly overridden.
    17	if [[ ! -v NIX_BITCOIN_FETCH_UPSTREAM ]]; then
    18	    echo "This is a fork of nix-bitcoin; pin the flake, don't fetch releases:" >&2
    19	    echo "  inputs.nix-bitcoin.url = \"github:ostermayer/nix-bitcoin/release\";" >&2
    20	    echo "This helper fetches UPSTREAM ($repo) releases, WITHOUT the fork's fixes." >&2
    21	    echo "Set NIX_BITCOIN_FETCH_UPSTREAM=1 only if you truly want upstream." >&2
    22	    exit 1
    23	fi
    24	
    25	if [[ ! -v version ]]; then
    26	    version=$(curl -fsS "https://api.github.com/repos/$repo/releases/latest" | jq -r '.tag_name' | tail -c +2)
    27	fi
    28	
    29	TMPDIR=$(mktemp -d)
    30	trap 'rm -rf $TMPDIR' EXIT
    31	
    32	export GNUPGHOME=$TMPDIR/gpg-home
    33	mkdir -m 700 "$GNUPGHOME"
    34	
    35	# Import key
    36	gpg --import "$scriptDir/key-jonasnick.bin" &> /dev/null
    37	# Check that exactly one key was imported
    38	(($(gpg --list-keys --with-colons | grep -c pub) == 1))
    39	# Verify key fingerprint
    40	gpg --list-keys "36C7 1A37 C9D9 88BD E825 08D9 B1A7 0E4F 8DCD 0366" > /dev/null
    41	
    42	# Fetch nar-hash of release
    43	cd "$TMPDIR"
    44	baseUrl=https://github.com/$repo/releases/download/v$version
    45	curl -fsS -L -O "$baseUrl/nar-hash.txt"
    46	curl -fsS -L -O "$baseUrl/nar-hash.txt.asc"
    47	
    48	# Verify signature for nar-hash
    49	gpg --verify nar-hash.txt.asc &> /dev/null || {
    50	    >&2 echo "Error: Signature verification failed. Please open an issue in the project repository."
    51	    exit 1
    52	}
    53	
    54	>&2 echo "Fetched and verified release $version"
    55	
    56	cat <<EOF
    57	builtins.fetchTarball {
    58	  url = "https://github.com/$repo/archive/v$version.tar.gz";
    59	  sha256 = "$(cat nar-hash.txt)";
    60	}
    61	EOF

     1	# Security-driven version overrides for packages whose pinned nixpkgs
     2	# version ships known vulnerabilities.
     3	#
     4	# Each override must be version-guarded so it turns inert once the pinned
     5	# nixpkgs catches up. Flake updates are then always safe to take.
     6	#
     7	# Example (from the clboss fund-theft fix, since resolved by
     8	# trimming clightning support from this fork):
     9	#
    10	#   clboss = if lagging pkgsUnstable.clboss "0.16.2" then pkgsUnstable.clboss.overrideAttrs (old: rec {
    11	#     version = "0.16.2";
    12	#     src = pkgs.fetchzip {
    13	#       url = "https://github.com/ZmnSCPxj/clboss/releases/download/v${version}/clboss-v${version}.tar.gz";
    14	#       hash = "...";
    15	#     };
    16	#   }) else pkgsUnstable.clboss;
    17	{ pkgs, pkgsUnstable }:
    18	let
    19	  inherit (pkgs) lib;
    20	  lagging = pkg: version: lib.versionOlder pkg.version version;
    21	in
    22	{
    23	  # 2.4.3 (2026-08-24) is a security release ("updating is recommended for
    24	  # servers shared with many users"); nixpkgs 26.05 carries 2.4.2. The nuget
    25	  # deps changed (HtmlSanitizer 9.1.982 -> 9.2.995 among others), so this is a
    26	  # full package copy with a regenerated lockfile (see ./btcpayserver/) —
    27	  # buildDotnetModule can't take nugetDeps via overrideAttrs. Drop the copy
    28	  # together with this override once the pin catches up.
    29	  btcpayserver = if lagging pkgs.btcpayserver "2.4.4"
    30	                 then pkgs.callPackage ./btcpayserver { }
    31	                 else pkgs.btcpayserver;
    32	}

    23	# build-to-cachix.sh early-exits green when a scenario's result is already in
    24	# the binary cache — so every push reports a real conclusion for all five
